Impact of the KWL reading strategy on mathematical problem-solving
page=3 block=1776312161 (x=1 y=62) width=436 height=8 fontname=HFABCD+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
achievement of primary school 4th graders
page=3 block=1776312161 (x=1 y=74) width=265 height=12 fontname=HFABCD+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
In this study, it was aimed to investigate possible impact of using the KWL reading strategy in
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=166) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
teaching the topic of “fractions and operations with fractions” to the 4th graders on students’
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=174) width=378 height=7 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problem-solving achievement. A quasi-experimental design with pretest-posttest control group
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=186) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
(CG) was employed in the study. The study group consisted of 35 4th graders studying at a pri-
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=194) width=378 height=7 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
mary school in Bart?n, Turkey. In the research, an experimental group (EG) and a control group
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=206) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(CG) were used. There were 18 students in the experimental group (EG) and 17 students in the
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=216) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
control group (CG). The test of equivalency (TE), Problem-Solving Achievement Test (PSAT) to
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=226) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
gauge effect of instruction with the KWL strategy and reading texts were employed as data collec-
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=236) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tion tools. The Wilcoxon Signed Rank Test and Mann Whitney U Test were employed in the
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=246) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
research. It was found as a result of the research that the KWL strategy fostered the students’
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=256) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problem-solving achievement. However, there were no significant differences between the EG and
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=266) width=378 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
CG in terms of the scores received in the step of implementing the plan.
page=3 block=1235381529 (x=13 y=276) width=279 height=5 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Bartin University, Bartin, Turkey.
page=3 block=1930319715 (x=216 y=704) width=101 height=4 fontname=HFABEG+AdvOT5fcf1b24 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

ABSTRACT
page=3 block=732688777 (x=13 y=157) width=37 height=4 fontname=HFABEH+AdvOT2bda31c3.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
Introduction
page=3 block=1201614274 (x=1 y=329) width=59 height=6 fontname=HFABEJ+AdvOT3b30f6db.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
Our age necessitates the development of individuals’ mental
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=348) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
skills such as understanding, sorting, classifying, questioning,
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=361) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
associating, predicting, analyzing, synthesizing and evaluat-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=373) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ing. Besides, raising individuals who are critical and creative
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=385) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
thinkers, who are entrepreneurs, who can express them-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=397) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
selves, who can communicate and who have advanced prob-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=409) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lem-solving skills is among educational policies of every
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=422) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
country. In recent years, various approaches have been pro-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=434) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
posed for curriculums in Turkey, and improvements have
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=446) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
been made from time to time. In this context (Ministry of
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=458) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
National Education (MoNE), MoNE, MoNE, 2013; MoNE,
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=471) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
2018), the skills required to be acquired in mathematics cur-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=483) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
riculum include communication, reasoning, association,
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=495) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
using information and communication technologies and
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=507) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
problem-solving. As at all levels of education, it is aimed to
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=519) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
develop problem-solving skills in primary education
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=532) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
(Baykul, 2014).
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=544) width=62 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Aim of this study was to examine impact of the KWL
page=3 block=229329107 (x=13 y=556) width=240 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
reading strategy on primary school 4th graders’ problem-
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=568) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
solving achievement.
page=3 block=229329107 (x=1 y=580) width=85 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Theoretical framework
page=3 block=92941132 (x=1 y=612) width=100 height=5 fontname=HFABFL+AdvOTd0125be5.BI wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Constructivism, which is a philosophical approach that
page=3 block=1901700557 (x=1 y=631) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
explains how individuals learn and understand, asserts the
page=3 block=1901700557 (x=1 y=643) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
idea that knowledge is not explored but interpreted, is not
page=3 block=1901700557 (x=1 y=655) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
revealed but created, that is, knowledge is constructed by
page=3 block=1901700557 (x=1 y=667) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
individuals. Knowledge is shaped as a result of one’s own
page=3 block=1901700557 (x=1 y=679) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

primary school students
page=3 block=2089104518 (x=409 y=238) width=76 height=4 fontname=HFABEG+AdvOT5fcf1b24 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
experiences, observations, interpretations and logical think-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=330) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ing (O€zden, 2009). Piaget, one of the pioneers of the con-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=340) width=252 height=7 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
structivist approach, explained knowledge construction as a
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=354) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
result of the active interaction of the individual with his
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=366) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
environment through successive processes called accommo-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=378) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
dation and assimilation (Baki, 2014). According to the con-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=390) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
structivist approach, learning is defined as “the process of
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=402) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
constructing meaning by connecting new knowledge with
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=414) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
old knowledge and experience”. The learner constructs his/
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=426) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
her own conceptualization and solutions of problems. This
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=438) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
approach acknowledges that students learn new knowledge
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=450) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
by adapting the information they have already learned to
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=462) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
their subjective situations (O€zden, 2009). In this context,
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=473) width=252 height=7 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
constructivism is based on Ausubel’s “Meaningful Learning
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=487) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Theory”. According to Ausubel, for a meaningful learning,
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=499) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the individual should have the correct prior knowledge
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=511) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
about the subject to be learned and be willing and deter-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=523) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
mined to learn, as well as associating the new knowledge
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=535) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and the prior knowledge (Asubel, 2000, as cited by
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=547) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=11
Meydan, 2018).
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=559) width=64 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
According to NCTM (2000), pupils should learn math-
page=3 block=390994793 (x=277 y=571) width=240 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ematics by understanding it and actively construct new
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=583) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
knowledge by making use of their prior knowledge and
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=595) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
experiences. Principles and Standards for School
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=607) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
Mathematics’s (2000) learning principle is based on two
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=619) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
basic thoughts the first of which is learning mathematics by
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=631) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
understanding it. Today’s mathematics requires not only
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=643) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
computational skills, but also the ability to reason and think
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=655) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in order to solve new problems, and to learn new ideas that
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=667) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
students will encounter in the future. The second is that
page=3 block=390994793 (x=265 y=680) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Page: 4 left:1 upper:1
344
page=4 block=273041802 (x=1 y=1) width=12 height=4 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
N. USTA AND M. YILMAZ
page=4 block=608108604 (x=36 y=1) width=79 height=4 fontname=HFABCE+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
students can learn mathematics by understanding. Learning
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=26) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
is developed in classrooms so that students can evaluate
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=38) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
their own and others’ ideas, and they are encouraged to gen-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=50) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
erate and test mathematical assumptions and to develop rea-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=62) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
soning skills (as cited by Van De Walle et al., 2013).
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=75) width=217 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Mathematics is commonly regarded as a difficult subject
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=13 y=87) width=240 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
to be understood by students. To learn mathematics, firstly
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=99) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
it should be understood. For this, it is significant for a stu-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=111) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
dent to understand what he/she reads and make out it.
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=123) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
There are some basic principles that should be taken into
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=135) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
account in addition to reading comprehension for under-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=148) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
standing mathematics. One of them is concept teaching. It is
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=160) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
impossible for students to learn mathematics without under-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=172) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
standing concepts. Understanding a concept is associating it
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=184) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
with existing concepts. The more a concept is associated
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=196) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
with the concepts it is related to, the better it is understood.
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=208) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Learning mathematics requires establishing a relationship
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=221) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
between conceptual knowledge and operational knowledge
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=233) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(Baykul, 2014). The development of mathematical thinking
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=245) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
depends on the conceptualization of mathematical know-

ledge and its use when necessary. Good knowledge of math-

ematical concepts facilitates to find unique solutions to new
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=281) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problems that have not been encountered before (O€zy?ld?r?m

Gu€m€s? & Umay, 2018). Mathematical models, various

proofing methods, and examples from mathematics itself
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=318) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and from daily life can be used to understand mathematics.
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=330) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Examples used in accordance with various teaching objec-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=342) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
tives enrich and strengthen learning (Kula & O€ren Vural,

2019). In addition, the concept of proof can be used as a

between the concepts (Knuth, 2002). On the other hand, in

the process of problem- solving, the student can get the

opportunity to see the implementation of mathematical con-

cepts and connect the concepts with the method of oper-

ation, algorithm and equation to be used for the solution

(Schoen & Charles, 2003, as cited by Baki, 2018). In the pro-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=452) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cess of problem-solving, the individual has to use his exist-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=464) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ing knowledge actively. In particular, it is necessary to use
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=476) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
conceptual and operational knowledge in association. For

students, learning mathematics is possible by connecting the
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=501) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
prior knowledge with new knowledge. When this does not
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=513) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
occur, it may not be possible to understand and learn math-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=525) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ematics. (Hiebert & Carpenter, 1992, as cited by Ozy?ld?r?m
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=536) width=252 height=7 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Gu€mu€s? & Umay, 2018).
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=549) width=99 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
According to Charles (1985), for solving a problem, the
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=13 y=561) width=240 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
student goes through cognitive processes such as under-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=574) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
standing the problem sentence, selecting the necessary data
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=586) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
for solution, generating a solution to the problem, and
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=598) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
deciding whether the solution is logical or not. In this pro-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=610) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
cess, the student gathers concepts and processes and applies
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=622) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
them to solve the problem (Bernardo, 1999, as cited by Baki,
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=634) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
2018). In the process of problem-solving, the student
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=647) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
becomes aware of his/her knowledge, investigates for solu-
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=659) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
tions to the problem by using his/her prior knowledge,
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=671) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
makes predictions and evaluates his/her solutions. In this
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=683) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
situation, the student uses his/her existing knowledge while
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=695) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
investigating for solutions to the problem, explains, analyzes,
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=707) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
organizes and makes sense of all information (Baki, 2018).
page=4 block=1909141817 (x=1 y=720) width=242 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The development of mathematical thinking depends on
page=4 block=372898106 (x=278 y=26) width=239 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the development of mathematical skills. Problem-solving is
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=38) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
an important mathematical skill, too. Understanding the
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=50) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
problem, generating a strategy for solution, implementing
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=62) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
the strategy, evaluating solutions and writing a similar prob-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=75) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
lem are the steps included in this process. Understanding
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=87) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the problem is the most important step of problem-solving
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=99) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
process. If a problem cannot be identified, a solution cannot
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=111) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
be suggested and the problem cannot be solved. In the step
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=123) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
of understanding the problem, the most important critical
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=136) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
behaviors are to write down the things given and asked in
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=148) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the problem, to explain the problem with the students’ own
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=160) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
expressions and to draw a figure or diagram explaining the
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=172) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problem (Baykul, 2016). It is highly critical for students to
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=184) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
understand what they read and to explain the problem in
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=196) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
their own words at this step.
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=209) width=118 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Not understanding the problem properly causes students
page=4 block=372898106 (x=278 y=221) width=239 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
to have problems. Stating the problem in the students’ own
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=233) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
words and reading it as required are two important sub-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=245) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
steps in understanding the problem (Baykul, 2016).
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=257) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=12
According to Ogle (1986), since the KWL reading strategy
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=269) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
includes activities related to determination of the students’
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=281) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
prior knowledge about the text to be read, what they know
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=294) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and what they want to know, the student will be able to
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=306) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
connect the old knowledge with the new one. According to
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=318) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Stephens and Brown (2000), for a student to connect the
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=330) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
knowledge before the reading process with the information

obtained after reading enables him/her to organize, integrate
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=355) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and summarize (as cited by Ulu et al., 2016). For this rea-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=367) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
son, based on the expressions of Ogle (1986) and Stephens
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=379) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and Brown (2000), the idea that the KWL reading strategy
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=391) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
would be effective in problem-solving was determined as the
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=403) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
aim of the study.
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=415) width=70 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A student’s solving a problem depends on his/her under-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=278 y=427) width=239 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
standing it well. For providing this, he/she needs to fully
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=440) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
comprehend what he/she reads. Reading comprehension
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=452) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
means that individuals reach a new learning as a result of
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=464) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
associating new information they encounter in the texts with
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=476) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
their prior knowledge (Y?lmaz, 2019a). Reading comprehen-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=488) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sion process occurs through conscious thinking, which helps
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=500) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the reader relate thinking processes, content of the text,
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=513) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
prior knowledge, expectations and reading purposes (Block,
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=525) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
2004, as cited by Erdem, 2016). Reading comprehension can
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=537) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
be regarded as a process of creating a new related situation
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=549) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
by linking the words with schemas in mind. In this process,
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=561) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
mental activities such as thinking on a text or problem,
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=574) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
searching for the reasons, drawing conclusion, interpreting,
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=586) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
analyzing, synthesizing and evaluating are influential
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=598) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=12
(Gunes?, 2000). KWL, one of the reading strategies, contains
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=610) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
activities to develop reading comprehension skill. The KWL
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=622) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
table used for this strategy is a reflective table indicating
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=634) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
what they know, what they want to know and what they
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=647) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
learned. Using this strategy helps the students think on a
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=659) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
topic before reading it and the teacher gauge what his/her
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=671) width=251 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
students know after reading (Nicely, 2015). The KWL strat-
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=683) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
egy is quite effective in developing reading proficiency. A
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=695) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
great number of studies have revealed that this strategy is
page=4 block=372898106 (x=266 y=707) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
useful for developing students’ reading skills and raising

KWL is one of the strategies used for improving reading

their background knowledge before reading and the know-

which the students listed their knowledge during implemen-

tation of the KWL strategy.

A three-column table is prepared before starting reading

of reaching a solution when the solution is unknown (Polya,

1962). Thus, mathematics, itself, is considered to be a prob-

included in curriculums as one of the basic approaches in

mathematics teaching ( MoNE, 2013, 2018; National Council

page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=136) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
is necessary to apply reasoning in the process of problem-

solving (Schoenfeld, 1985). Reasoning is the process of
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=160) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
thinking by considering all factors and obtaining a reason-

able result. Individuals who are able to reason about a topic
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=184) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
have sufficient knowledge in that topic, can examine new
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=196) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
situation encountered thoroughly, explore, guess, infer, reach
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=209) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to some results by justifying thoughts, explain these results
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=221) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and offer solutions (Umay, 2003). Hence, a problem cannot
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=233) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
be solved unless it is understood well, and mathematical
page=7 block=1966114624 (x=265 y=245) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ideas cannot be defended without showing reasons.

current problem, similar problems and solutions. In this case,

it means that the student has established a link between his/

page=13 block=1932303196 (x=10 y=119) width=72 height=5 fontname=HFABEG+AdvOT5fcf1b24 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
between the scores of the EG and CG students. Non-para-
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=254) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
metric techniques were employed for data analysis since
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=267) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
the number of subjects in the groups was small. Mann-
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=279) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Whitney U test checks if the scores obtained from two
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=292) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
independent samples differ significantly. This test controls
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=304) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
if two independent groups have similar distributions in the
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=317) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
population in terms of the concerned variable. U test
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=329) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
requires the dependent variable to be in ordinal scale, and
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=342) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
observations need to be independent of each other.
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=354) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=11
Wilcoxon’s signed ranks test is used to test significance of
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=367) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the difference between the scores belonging to two depend-
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=379) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ent measuring sets. This test is used as an alternative for t-
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=392) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
test in two paired groups when variables do not show nor-
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=404) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
mal distribution (B€y€k€ztu€rk, 2013). The Wilcoxon’s
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=417) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Signed Rank Test for the analyses between the dependent
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=429) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
groups and The Mann-Whitney U Test for the analyses
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=442) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
between the independent groups were implemented. This
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=454) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
method was preferred since the data obtained from the
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=467) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
research were not distributed normally, and the study
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=479) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
group was small.
page=13 block=942804532 (x=1 y=492) width=70 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Findings
page=13 block=1030384622 (x=1 y=524) width=40 height=6 fontname=HFABEJ+AdvOT3b30f6db.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
In this section of the research, the findings and interpreta-
page=13 block=920497855 (x=1 y=543) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tions were included. Firstly, the results of the test of equiva-

Discussion and conclusion
page=16 block=1522975986 (x=1 y=303) width=125 height=6 fontname=HFABEJ+AdvOT3b30f6db.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In the present study, impact of the KWL reading strategy on
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=322) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
graders was investigated. It was found in this study, in
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=347) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
which a quasi-experimental design with pretest-posttest CG
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=359) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
was employed, that the KWL strategy improved the stu-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=371) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
dents’ problem-solving achievement as a result of non-para-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=383) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
metric tests.
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=395) width=50 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Each step of the problem-solving process was separately
page=16 block=715534618 (x=13 y=407) width=240 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
analyzed. Accordingly, there was a significant difference in
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=419) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
favor of the EG in each step of problem-solving which are
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=431) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
understanding the problem, devising a plan, looking back
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=443) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and suggesting a problem. Considering the sum of the
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=455) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
scores obtained from each step, a statistically significant dif-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=467) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ference was found in problem-solving achievement of the
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=479) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
two groups in favor of the EG. However, no significant dif-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=491) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ference was found between the scores of the EG and CG
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=503) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
students in the step of carrying out the plan.
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=515) width=185 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A significant difference was observed between the sum of
page=16 block=715534618 (x=13 y=527) width=240 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the scores received from each step by the EG and CG stu-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=539) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
dents in favor of the former ones as a result of the within-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=551) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
groups analyses. On the other hand, no significant differen-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=563) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ces were found between the pretest and posttest scores
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=575) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
received in the steps of understanding the problem, looking
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=587) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
back and suggesting a problem by the CG students when
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=599) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
each step was separately analyzed.
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=611) width=140 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Understanding the problem is the most important step of
page=16 block=715534618 (x=13 y=623) width=240 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problem-solving. This stems from the fact that a student
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=635) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
who cannot understand the problem cannot choose and
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=647) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
carry out an appropriate strategy for solving the problem
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=659) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and cannot interpret the results. The current research
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=671) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
revealed how important the step of understanding the prob-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=684) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
lem for solving a problem is. Within-group test results indi-
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=696) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
cated that the results could not be appropriately interpreted
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=708) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by the CG students, to whom the instruction in accordance
page=16 block=715534618 (x=1 y=720) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
problem-solving achievement of the primary school 4th

for solving the problem (Bernardo, 1999). Therefore, it can

page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=321) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Ulu et al. (2016) claimed that reading comprehension strat-

egies could explain just 7,86% of the change in problem-
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=346) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
solving achievement, and students’ readiness level were
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=358) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
more influential in predicting problem-solving achievement
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=370) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
than their reading comprehension strategies. Thus, it cannot
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=383) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
be asserted that the results of the current study conform
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=395) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
with the results of the study carried out by Ulu et al. (2016).
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=407) width=251 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The current study employed quantitative methods to

examine the problem-solving achievements of the primary

school 4th graders, each step of which was analyzed separ-
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=441) width=252 height=9 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ately, with the KWL reading strategy. A qualitative study
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=456) width=252 height=6 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
can be carried out in order to obtain in-depth information
page=17 block=1127866590 (x=265 y=469) width=252 height=5 fontname=HFABEK+AdvOT1ef757c0 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
about reasons of these results. In addition, mixed studies in

which factors affecting the problem-solving skill by using
